L3297: The calculated AUR values are less than 1.25, thereby suggesting that BIC is not expected to be an inhibitor of the CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A.
L3298: *Time Dependent Inhibition by BIC:*
L3299: The results of study AD-141-2308 showed that BIC is not a time dependent inhibitor of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19 or CYP2D6. There was an indication of time dependent inhibition of CYP3A, however, the potency was too weak to allow calculation of enzyme inactivation parameters (Kiu &gt; 98.4 µM).
L3300: *Induction:*
L3301: Per the FDA and PMDA guidance, the metric is calculated as follows:
L3302: \text{metric} = \frac{1}{1 + \frac{d \times C_{max} \times E_{max}}{C_{max} + EC_{50}}}
L3303: Where a value of &lt; 0.9 is considered positive in both guidance documents. Further, if the E_{max} value is determined in hepatocyte studies (fold increase in mRNA above the vehicle control), then it must be corrected for “1-fold” induction to yield E_{max} increase (E_{max}=E_{max\text{hepatocytes}}-1).
L3304: The intestinal and hepatic components of the net effect model are calculated in the same manner in all Guidance documents. For the FDA Guidance they are termed C_g and C_h, respectively, while for the EMA and PMDA Guidance documents they are termed B_g and B_h, respectively.
L3305: \text{Intestinal} = 1 + \frac{d \times [I]_g \times E_{max}}{[I]_g + EC_{50}} \quad \text{Hepatic} = 1 + \frac{d \times [I]_h \times E_{max}}{[I]_h + EC_{50}}
L3306: **Table 10: Calculation Results for Induction Liability of BIC**
L3307: Target	Hepatocyte Data	Basic
R_3 or R	Net Effect
EC_{50,u} (\mu\text{M})	E_{max}^a	d	Intestinal	Hepatic	AUCR
CYP1A2	No induction	16.3	0.90	\ge 0.9	1.0	1.0	1.0
CYP2B6	102.8	10.5	1.04	0.45	2.39	1.00	1.0
CYP3A4	19.1	22.7	0.40	0.21 / 0.10b	5.1 / 11.2b	1.02 / 1.05b	0.36 / 0.16b
L3308: Values exceeding the respective threshold are in bold
L3309: a Corrected for baseline (1-fold) increase in mRNA
L3310: b Value calculated using d = 0.40 / value calculated using d = 1.0
L3311: Source: Clinical Study Report, Page 18
L3312: For CYP2B6, the calculated AUCR is 1.0 (no difference when using plasma f_u of 0.25% or plasma f_u of 1.0%) so bictegravir will not cause clinically relevant drug interactions through induction of CYP2B6. For CYP3A the calculated AUCR is 0.36 using experimentally determined values (d = 0.40 and plasma f_u 0.25%) and drops to 0.18 using Guidance defaults of d = 1.0 and plasma f_u = 1.0 %. Both values cross the threshold of \text{AUCR} &lt; 0.8 so BIC may induce CYP3A enzymes *in vivo*, however, the results of the *in vivo* trial conducted with midazolam (GS-US-380-4270) did not suggest the potential for BIC to induce CYP enzymes *in vivo*.
L3313: ## *Inhibition of Intestinal Efflux Transporters:*
L3314: The three guidances use a similar metric for determining significance. The FDA and PMDA Guidance documents represents this as [I]/IC_{50} (or K_i) \ge 10, where [I] is variously denoted by [I]_2, [I]_g or [I]_2, while the EMA Guidance represents this as K_i \le 0.1 \times [I]_{\text{gut}}.
L3315: **Table 11: Calculation Results for Intestinal Efflux Transporter Interactions for BIC**
L3316: Transporter	IC50,u (\muM)	Ki,u (\muM)	FDA [I]_2/K_{i,u}	EMA 0.1 \times [I]_{\text{gut}} (\muM)	PMDA [I]_2/K_{i,u}
P-gp	> 80	> 40	11.1	44.5	11.1
BCRP	> 80	> 40	11.1	44.5	11.1
L3317: Values potentially exceeding the respective threshold are in bold
L3318: Source: Clinical Study Report, Page 18
L3319: Under the assumption that the IC50,u is equal to the maximum concentration tested (80 \muM), BIC would slightly exceed the thresholds for both transporters. However, it should be noted that inhibition observed at the highest bicitgravir concentration tested (80 \muM) was only 20% for P-gp and 6% for BCRP (AD-141-2273) so the Ki,u values for both transporters are likely to be much greater than 40 \muM. Overall, the potential for BIC to inhibit P-gp and BCRP is very low.
L3320: ## *Inhibition of Hepatic Uptake Transporters by BIC:*
L3321: There was no detectable inhibition of OATP1B1 *in vitro* so BIC is not considered to be an inhibitor of this transporter. The FDA Guidance suggests an initial computation of Cmax/IC50, with ratios \ge 0.1 subject to calculation of R values as follows:
L3322: \text{FDA metric: } R = 1 + \frac{[I]_{\text{in,max,u}}}{K_{i,u}} \ge 1.25
L3323: \text{EMA metric: } K_{i,u} \le 25 \times [I]_h
L3324: \text{PMDA metric} = \frac{[I]_{\text{inlet,max,u}}}{K_{i,u}} \ge 0.25 \text{ or } R = 1 + \frac{[I]_{\text{inlet,max,u}}}{K_{i,u}} \ge 1.25
L3325: **Table 12: Calculation Results for Hepatic Uptake Transporter Interactions of BIC**
L3326: Transporter	IC50,u (\muM)	Ki,u (\muM)	FDA [I]_h/K_{i,u}	FDA R	EMA 25 \times [I]_h (\muM)	PMDA R
OATP1B1	Not an inhibitor	< 0.1	< 1.25	2.9	< 1.25
OATP1B3	> 80	> 40	< 0.34	1.0	2.9	1.0
OCT1	> 100	> 50	< 0.27	1.0	2.9	1.0
L3327: Values potentially exceeding the respective threshold are in bold
L3328: Source: Clinical Study Report, Page 19
L3329: For OATP1B3 and OCT1, the R values (calculated as described in the FDA and PMDA guidance) are &lt; 1.01, and Ki,u values are \ge 13.8-fold higher than the EMA 25 \times [I]_h test (all calculated using the most conservative values of [I]_{\text{in,max,u}} and [I]_h with plasma fu or blood fu,b = 1.0%). Thus, BIC would not be expected to inhibit any of the hepatic uptake transporters.
L3330: ## *Inhibition of Hepatic Efflux and Renal Transporters by BIC:*
L3331: The metrics, as described in the three guidance documents, were calculated:
L3332: FDA \text{ metric renal} : \frac{C_{\max,u}}{K_{i,u}} \ge 0.1
L3333: PMDA \text{ metric renal} : K_{i,u} \le 4 \times C_{\max,u} \text{ or } 1 + \frac{C_{\max,u}}{K_{i,u}} \ge 1.25 \quad FDA \text{ and } PMDA \text{ metric hepatic} : \frac{C_{\max}}{K_{i,u}} \ge 0.1
L3334: EMA \text{ metric} : K_{i,u} \le 50 \times C_{\max,u}
L3335: **Table 13: Calculation Results from Hepatic Efflux Transporter and Renal Transporter Interactions for BIC**
L3336: Transporter	K_{i,u} (\muM)	FDA [I_u]/K_{i,u}	FDA C_{\max,u}/K_{i,u}a	EMA 50 \times C_{\max,u} (\muM)b	PMDA [I_u]/K_{i,u}	PMDA 1 + C_{\max,u}/K_{i,u}a
OAT1	Not an inhibitor	NA	< 0.1	1.7 / 6.9	NA	< 1.25
OAT3	27.5	NA	0.001 / 0.005	1.7 / 6.9	NA	1.001 / 1.005
OCT1	> 50	NA	< 0.001 / < 0.003	1.7 / 6.9	NA	< 1.001 / < 1.003
OCT2	0.21	NA	0.16 / 0.65	1.7 / 6.9	NA	1.16 / 1.65
MATE1	4.0	NA	0.01 / 0.03	1.7 / 6.9	NA	1.01 / 1.03
Pgp	> 80	< 0.34	NA	1.7 / 6.9	< 0.34	NA
BCRP	> 80	< 0.34	NA	1.7 / 6.9	< 0.34	NA
BSEP	> 100	< 0.27	NA	1.7 / 6.9	< 0.27	NA
L3337: NA: Not Applicable (transporter does not fall within that classification)
L3338: Values potentially exceeding the respective threshold are in bold
L3339: a Value calculated using plasma f_u = 0.25\% / value calculated using plasma f_u = 1.0\%
L3340: Source: Clinical Study Report, Page 20
L3341: Based on the calculations shown in the table above, BIC is not expected to be an inhibitor of OAT1, OAT3 and OCT1, but expected to inhibit OCT2. If it is assumed that IC_{50,u} is equal to the maximum concentration tested (80 \muM) BIC would exceed the FDA and PMDA thresholds for P-gp and BCRP inhibition. However, it should be noted that inhibition observed at the highest bictegravir concentration tested (80 \muM) was only 20% for P-gp and 6% for BCRP (AD-141-2273) so the K_{i,u} values for both transporters are likely to be much greater than 40 \muM.
L3342: ## Conclusions:
L3343: - At clinically relevant concentrations, BIC would not be expected to cause drug-drug interactions with substrates of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, OAT1, OAT3, OCT1, OATP1B1 or OATP1B3.
- Although the predictions suggest the potential for BIC to induce CYP3A and possibly be a time dependent inhibitor of CYP3A4 (albeit the potency was too weak to allow calculation of enzyme inactivation parameters [K_{i,u} &gt; 98.4 \mu\text{M}]), the results of the *in vivo* trial conducted with midazolam (GS-US-380-4270) did not suggest the potential for BIC to induce CYP enzymes *in vivo*.
- BIC is expected to inhibit OCT2 transporters *in vivo* which was confirmed by the results of the drug-drug interaction trial with metformin, a substrate of OCT2 and MATE transporters (GS-US-380-3908).

L3360: ## Pharmacology/Toxicology Review
L3361: **Goal Date:** November 12, 2017
L3362: **Completion Date:** October 30, 2017
L3363: **NDA #:** 210251
L3364: **Supporting document#:** None
L3365: **CDER stamp date(s):** June 12, 2017
L3366: **Product name:** BIKTARVY
L3367: **Indication:** Treatment of HIV-1 infection in adults who are HIV-1 treatment-
resistance to the individual components of BIKTARVY(b) (4) associated with
L3368: **Applicant:** Gilead Sciences
L3369: **Reviewer:** John Dubinion, Ph.D.
L3370: **Recommendation:** It is recommended that BIKTARVY be approved.
L3371: **Summary:** Biktarvy™, a once daily fixed-dose combination (FDC) tablet containing bictegravir (BIC; B), emtricitabine (FTC; F), and tenofovir alafenamide (TAF), is intended for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adults. Bictegravir (previously referred to as GS-9883) is a potent integrase strand-transfer inhibitor (INSTI) that blocks HIV-1 replication, and is the only component of the FDC which has not been previously approved for use by the FDA. FTC and TAF have been approved for marketing in the U.S. as standalone agents (Emtriva® and Vemlidy®, respectively) or in multiple FDC products (Genvoya®, Descovy®, and Odefsey®).
L3372: The nonclinical safety profile of BIC was evaluated in: safety pharmacology studies in rats and monkeys; repeat-dose toxicology studies in mice, rats and monkeys for up to 4, 26, and 39 weeks, respectively; at least 2-week toxicology studies in rats to qualify impurities; phototoxicity studies in mouse fibroblasts and pigmented rats; fertility and pre- and post-natal developmental studies in rats; embryo-fetal developmental studies in rats and rabbits; genetic toxicology studies (Ames, *in vitro* chromosomal aberration and *in vivo* rat micronucleus assays); and a carcinogenicity study in transgenic mice. In addition, numerous *in vitro* and *in vivo* nonclinical pharmacokinetic studies evaluating the absorption, distribution, metabolism and excretion of BIC have been conducted in mice, rats, dogs, and monkeys, and a rat carcinogenicity study with BIC is currently in progress. Nonclinical safety studies for F/TAF to support the FDC were reviewed previously. Refer to the Pharmacology/Toxicology reviews for NDA-021500, NDA-208464, and NDA-208215 for a detailed summary of FTC, TAF, and F/TAF nonclinical data, respectively. Pharmacology/Toxicology Review is complete, and has been added to the NDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation. Pharm/Tox recommendation for this application is approval.

L3385: Figure 7 is a study schema for Trial 1878, illustrating the timeline from Screening through Week 48 and a 30 Day Follow-Up (F/U).
The timeline starts with Screening (≤ 30 days prior to Day 1). At Day 1, subjects are randomized into two groups:
Treatment Group 1: GS-9883/F/TAF FDC QD (n=260)
Treatment Group 2: Remain on current regimen of boosted ATV or DRV plus FTC/TDF or ABC/3TC QD (n=260)
Both groups continue treatment until Week 48. After Week 48, subjects in countries where GS-9883/F/TAF FDC is not available may receive it for an additional 48 weeks (up to 96 weeks total) or until the product becomes accessible through an access program, whichever occurs first. Subjects who complete the study through the Week 48 Visit and do not wish to receive GS-9883/F/TAF FDC will be required to return for a 30-Day Follow-Up Visit.

The X-axis lists "CYP Enzyme" (1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, 3A4, 3A5). The chart shows that CYP 1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, and 2D6 exhibit minimal metabolism (Substrate Remaining > 90%). In contrast, CYP 3A4 and 3A5 show significant metabolism, with Substrate Remaining around 55% and 35% respectively.
L3411: Figure 2 displays two graphs showing the inhibition of P-gp and BCRP-mediated transport. The left graph shows P-gp inhibition by GS-9883, and the right graph shows P-gp inhibition by Verapamil. Both P-gp graphs plot % of Inhibition (Y-axis, 0 to 100) against concentration (X-axis, 10^{-1} to 10^2 \muM). GS-9883 shows weak inhibition, reaching approximately 20% at 10^2 \muM. Verapamil shows strong inhibition, reaching 100% inhibition at 10^1 \muM. The bottom row of the figure shows BCRP inhibition by GS-9883 (left) and Fumitremorgin C (right). Both BCRP graphs plot % of Inhibition (Y-axis, 0 to 100) against concentration (X-axis, 10^{-1} to 10^2 \muM). GS-9883 shows minimal inhibition, reaching approximately 5% at 10^2 \muM. Fumitremorgin C shows strong inhibition, reaching 100% inhibition at 10^1 \muM.
L3412: Figure 2 displays two graphs showing the inhibition of BCRP-mediated transport. The left graph shows BCRP inhibition by GS-9883, and the right graph shows BCRP inhibition by Fumitremorgin C. Both BCRP graphs plot % of Inhibition (Y-axis, 0 to 100) against concentration (X-axis, 10^{-1} to 10^2 \muM). GS-9883 shows minimal inhibition, reaching approximately 5% at 10^2 \muM. Fumitremorgin C shows strong inhibition, reaching 100% inhibition at 10^1 \muM.
L3413: Figure 1 displays two graphs showing the inhibition of OATP1B1 mediated transport of 2 \muM Fluo 3 in CHO cells by GS-9883 (BIC) and Rifampin (positive control).
The left graph (OATP1B1) shows % of Uptake versus GS-9883 concentration (\muM). Uptake is high (around 100%) across the tested range (10^{-6} to 10^2 \muM), indicating minimal inhibition by GS-9883.
The right graph (OATP1B1) shows % of Uptake versus Rifampin concentration (\muM). Uptake is high (around 100%) at low concentrations (10^{-6} to 10^{-2} \muM), and then decreases sharply as Rifampin concentration increases, reaching near 0% uptake at 10^2 \muM, demonstrating dose-dependent inhibition.
L3414: Figure 2 displays two graphs showing the inhibition of OATP1B3 mediated transport of 2 \muM Fluo 3 in CHO cells by GS-9883 (BIC) and Rifampin (positive control).
The left graph (OATP1B3) shows % of Uptake versus GS-9883 concentration (\muM). Uptake is high (around 100%) across the tested range (10^{-6} to 10^2 \muM), indicating minimal inhibition by GS-9883.
The right graph (OATP1B3) shows % of Uptake versus Rifampin concentration (\muM). Uptake is high (around 100%) at low concentrations (10^{-6} to 10^{-2} \muM), and then decreases sharply as Rifampin concentration increases, reaching near 0% uptake at 10^2 \muM, demonstrating dose-dependent inhibition.
L3415: Figure 2 displays two graphs showing TEA Uptake (% of control) versus inhibitor concentration for OCT2 transporters. Both graphs show a concentration-dependent inhibition curve, where TEA uptake decreases as the inhibitor concentration increases.
The left graph shows inhibition by GS-9883. The x-axis is GS-9883 concentration (\muM) on a logarithmic scale from 10^{-6} to 10^2. The y-axis is TEA Uptake (% of control) from 0 to 100. TEA uptake is near 100% at low concentrations and drops sharply above 10^{-2} \muM.
The right graph shows inhibition by dolutegravir. The x-axis is dolutegravir concentration (\muM) on a logarithmic scale from 10^{-6} to 10^2. The y-axis is TEA Uptake (% of control) from 0 to 100. TEA uptake is near 100% at low concentrations and drops sharply above 10^{-2} \muM.
L3416: Figure 2 (continued) displays a graph showing TEA Uptake (% of control) versus GS-9883 concentration (\muM) on a logarithmic scale from 0.1 to 100. The y-axis is TEA Uptake (% of control) from 0 to 120. The curve shows a concentration-dependent inhibition of TEA uptake, starting near 100% at low concentrations and decreasing significantly as GS-9883 concentration increases, reaching approximately 25% uptake at 100 \muM. Error bars are included.
L3417: Figure 1 displays two bar charts showing Midazolam 1'-hydroxylation (pmol/min/mg protein) versus inhibitor concentration. The left chart, labeled CYP3A4/5, compares 0.1% Methanol Control (approx. 700 pmol/min/mg protein) and 0.075 µM Ketoconazole (approx. 150 pmol/min/mg protein). The right chart, labeled CYP3A4/5, compares two conditions for 0.1% Acetonitrile Control (approx. 700 pmol/min/mg protein) and 7.5 µM Troleandomycin (approx. 400 pmol/min/mg protein). The legend indicates Zero-minute preincubation (solid bars) and 30-Minute preincubation plus NADPH (hatched bars).
L3418: Figure 2 is a graph showing Activity Remaining (% Vehicle control) versus Concentration (μM) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 100 μM). Three conditions are plotted: No preincubation (blue circles), Preincubation -NADPH (green squares), and Preincubation +NADPH (red triangles). The No preincubation curve shows the highest activity remaining, starting around 110% at 0.1 μM and dropping to about 55% at 100 μM. The Preincubation -NADPH curve shows activity remaining around 95-100% across the concentration range. The Preincubation +NADPH curve shows the lowest activity remaining, starting around 95% at 0.1 μM and dropping to about 25% at 100 μM. A dashed line indicates 50% activity remaining.
